The Týn church epitaph, Prague — the effigy that closes the seriesIs the globe terrestrial or celestial?open: one verified witness each way (Studnička 1901 celestial; Vellev 2006 Danish text terrestrial); the 2006 English summary is neutral, so the volume does not contradict itself; no surrogate — including the 1894 pre-restoration engraving — shows surface markings on the globe; and Herain's 1901 grinding means the present surface cannot arbitrate the original
The small gilt brass quadrant — an iconographic instrumentWhat the emblem showsthe 2006 book is wrong; there is no astrolabe in the emblem
The printed views of Uraniborg and StjerneborgEngraving or woodcut?The east-facade view is a new copperplate (Beckett's 'Kobberstikkeren', p. 10); the Stjerneborg views are woodcuts; the instrument plates are mixed woodcut and engraving (Nielsen's collation of entry 432 tags the volume 'Træsn. og Kobberstik'; Horáček 2021 names three new 1598 instrument engravings). Open tension: Beckett assigns the fol. 84 ground-plan to the reused woodblocks, but its line quality in the KB scan matches the engraved facade — possibly design reused, block re-engraved
The printed views of Uraniborg and StjerneborgDate of the Stjerneborg viewsBeckett's block-reuse claim is now page-verified ('Klodserne … benyttede', p. 10; 'gentagne', p. 25) and extends to the Hven map, bird's-eye and ground-plan: 1598 dates the printing, not the image, for all but the east facade. The pixel-level defect-match remains undone — no true 1596 Epistolae digitisation was locatable (e-rara Rar 4403 is the 1601 Hulsius reissue; the census-confirmed 1596 Wolfenbüttel copy has no surrogate URL)
The printed views of Uraniborg and StjerneborgThe L+B monogram on the Epistolae east-facade viewundecided on present evidence; the cutter is unidentified — Nagler and Hollstein have not been checked (an omission, not a researched negative), and Lambert Cornelisz does not fit on initials or floruit
The posthumous copies (1602 onward)Does every seventeenth-century image descend from the two Amsterdam plates?the universal claim was too strong as written. The Rudolphine lineage shows Tycho as proprietor of a world system rather than as a face; whether it is fully independent of de Gheyn or carries a de Gheyn-derived face on an invented body is undetermined — no visual collation exists either way
Tycho's portrait on the celestial globes — a distribution channel begun in his lifetimePiety or marketing?Mosley governs the origin — lifetime, sanctioned, privilege-documented; Grinder-Hansen's piety reading remains defensible for the posthumous continuation, which the record's own latest specimens embody, and the 1613 Veen/Hondius globe shows the convention self-propagating after 1601.
The full-figure watercolour at Strahov — the standing Tycho with a sextantIs the drawing the model for Kilian's engraving, or a copy of it?unresolved, and it decides whether this is a lifetime object or a seventeenth-century one — the engraving is now recorded separately as icon-kilian-standing, with its plate at Wellcome 1357i, so the comparison Friis made by eye can be made properly as soon as an image of the watercolour is obtained. Dreyer endorses the model reading twice — Tycho Brahe (1890), printed p. 264, the sole occurrence in the book (the circulating citation 'notes p. 288' is an error), and again, unhedged, in his preface to Opera Omnia X (1923, via Meddelelser fra Ole Rømers Venner 14/1, 2006, p. 31) — but both endorsements root in the single 1881 Šafařík report: endorsement, not independent evidence. The same-sense finding recorded under nature leans against the model reading, carried with its two caveats.
The full-figure watercolour at Strahov — the standing Tycho with a sextantDoes the Studnička wrapper reproduce the watercolour, or Kilian's engraving?well-supported but with undocumented chain of custody — Friis's differentiators (plain slab, facsimile 'Tycho Brahe') are his own observations from a copy of the 1881 photolithograph in hand, absent from the Vierteljahrsschrift minutes he quotes, and neither feature can be produced by cropping or retouching the engraving; whether the wrapper prints from the 1881 plate or a fresh reproduction of the same original remains undocumented
Kilian's standing Tycho (1666) — a whole body invented around a borrowed headIs the Strahov watercolour Kilian's model, or a copy after his plate?still open, but rebalanced: documented testimony — Kraus and Studnička via Friis's addendum, and Studnička 1901's silence on the watercolour among his catalogued genuine Tychoniana — now leans against Strahov-as-model, without reaching dispositive: the reversal is method-less hearsay about an object already missing when it was reported, and collides with Studnička's own printed 'preserved'. The question still decides whether the Strahov watercolour is a lifetime object or a seventeenth-century one.

Standing questions of the edition 9

  1. The 1595 medal: existence is now settled (Mortensen, Dreyer, Beckett; Galster 53) — what is still wanted is a photograph of an actual specimen and a check of the British Museum's holdings, which could not be checked. Public-cabinet specimens are often aftercasts.
  2. The Cornelisz print's surviving impressions: the ÖNB Bildarchiv (the scanned mount suggests Vienna) and the Royal Library's print room are the two places to ask; Friis reportedly saw an impression in Vienna in 1868.
  3. The drawing's verso: microscopy of the compound corner mark (whether there is ink under the forged red-chalk HG), raking light for the needle incisions, and the date of the perimeter line — a single study-room session at SMK would answer all three.
  4. The Kronborg tapestry identification: Wanscher's conjecture has not been tested against the likeness family, and the companion figure's sphere — a globe? — deserves a focused look.
  5. The pendant jewel over an elephant form — the one costume element that survives every revision (both engravings, both dies): what exactly was it? Tycho held no Order-of-the-Elephant membership.
  6. What were the diary's 4 May 1596 'Pictor' and Blaeu's 24 May 1596 departure actually for, now that the second engraving no longer needs them? The aetatis-50 standing type is a candidate.
  7. The Prague (Clementinum) panel: not yet examined at object level. Whether the Berlin photogravure really reproduces it is unconfirmed — Mortensen's plate number reads 3279 in his printed caption against 8279 on the sheet, and the panel's inscription needs to be read against the faint lettering visible at the sheet's upper left.
  8. The Steensgaard panel, original or copy — the one question in this edition that a single technical test would close. Mortensen's own 'Den ældste Tycho Brahe-portræt' (Nordisk astronomisk tidsskrift, 1935), never consulted, is very likely where he argued the case; Frederiksborg's iconographic register and the 1938 auction catalogues would show where the panel went; and if it is found, dendrochronology would date the oak.
  9. Which engraving sits in a given 1596 Epistolae copy (insertion was loose): only a copy-by-copy check of digitised copies can tell.

Open questions by record 76

The same questions the records carry, gathered so that a visit to one institution or one reading room can close several at once.

RecordQuestions
The Steensgaard panel — a lifetime portrait or an early copy, never tested
  1. The accession date and provenance of A 8191, which only the museum's own registry can supply: an acquisition in or after 1938 from the Bille-Brahe-Selby dispersal would close the identification. The national Kunstindeks Danmark is no substitute — Frederiksborg is absent from its roster of museums, and a museum filter there returns no artwork records.
  2. Harald Mortensen, 'Den ældste Tycho Brahe-portræt', Nordisk astronomisk tidsskrift n.s. xvi (1935), p. 58 — not yet consulted. The title matches his superlative for this panel exactly, and it predates both his 1937 'Originalmaleri (?)' and his 1946 'ældre Kopi'. No digitisation has been found (ADS, Runeberg, tidsskrift.dk and the Internet Archive are all negative; the 1934 Norwegian companion piece, read at nb.no, is silent on Steensgaard), so the article can only be read in the Royal Library.
  3. The 1938 dispersal catalogue, Fortegnelse over indbo fra Steensgaard (V. Winkel & Magnussen, auction 20 October 1938): a lot entry would give dimensions, support, attribution and the buyer. Copies are in the trade and in the Royal Library's auction-catalogue files. One caution: a trade listing transcribes the title-page decedent as 'Baron Preben Bille-Brahe-Selby' against the documented Hendrik lensgreve — most likely a transcription error, but not settled until the title page is seen.
  4. Daniel Hvidt's two painted copies — M/S Museet for Søfart, Helsingør, and the Astronomisk-Historisk Samling at Rundetårn — rest on Mortensen 1946 alone and have not been confirmed with the institutions.
  5. Should the panel be located, dendrochronology, then infrared reflectography and X-radiography, would settle lifetime against later and whether the astronomical attributes are restoration overpaint, as Mortensen suggested.
The Frederiksborg oil (attributed to Gemperlin) — known through C. A. Jensen's 1838 copy
  1. H. D. Schepelern, Portrætsamlingen i Odense adelige Jomfrukloster (1959) — the dedicated catalogue of exactly this collection, cited by Bruun Hansen. Not yet consulted for this edition. It is where a catalogue number, an exact measurement and a condition note would be found.
The Prague panel — Mortensen's Clementinum portrait
  1. No Czech institutional record for the panel has been reached — Klementinum, Národní technické muzeum, or the National Library's own catalogue.
  2. The Photographische Gesellschaft's own series catalogue or caption for plate 8279 — what the Berlin firm said the sheet reproduces — is the most decisive source not yet consulted.
  3. The entry text of Burgess 1973, no. 405.7 — the only named scholarly authority behind Wellcome's 'after J. Falck' title — remains unread.
  4. Hollstein's Falck entries, and Strunk's catalogue cited by Friis, have not been checked for a second Tycho plate beyond Block 214.
  5. A page-image read of Mortensen's printed plate number (Cassiopeia 8, p. 62) to settle 3279 vs 8279 at the print level.
  6. The Národní muzeum album-amicorum watercolour (Wittenberg 1598) as a possible referent behind a 'Prague painted portrait' citation — Studnička's only painted likeness.
  7. Westergaard vol. I, pp. 200 ff. (entries 1383–1431), remains to be read in full, for the text of W.1394 and the Falck entry W.1431.
The gilt portrait supralibros — two dies
  1. Does either physical die survive? Unchecked channels are Nationalmuseet's object database and Frederiksborg's iconographic register, neither of which is likely to hold a binding tool. The documentary silence now runs back past Gigas to Resen 1668 and Danske Magazin 1746 — stamped covers described, never a tool.
  2. Per-volume die-size assignment for Kleinschnitzová's six Clementinum shelfmarks (8 H 39, 12 H 10, 14 G 30, 14 J 176, 19 C 92, 9 B 135) — the decisive test of the format hypothesis, with predictions on record (octavos small, quartos large).
  3. Reconciliation of the Bodleian SOLO full-livery description of the Savile copy against the photographed 4° T 2 Art.Seld. Sammelband, and recovery of the Weston Savilian shelfmark — one object with a misattached description, or a seventh De mundi carrier?
  4. A first-hand raking-light reading of both gilt legends on the Sascerides copy (LN 428 4°, eks. 1), illegible in the digitised surrogate.
  5. A caliper reading of the de Lode plate. Danske Magazin II (1746) claims to render the portrait die at 1:1 and the arms die at 'selv samme Form og Størrelse'; measuring the oval on a physical 1746 sheet — or on a scan with a known scale — is the cheapest independent test of the large die's dimensions anywhere in this edition, and the only one that does not measure a compressed impression on leather or vellum.
Lambert Cornelisz's print (Westergaard 1409) — the copy Tycho disliked
  1. The holding institution of the impression reproduced here is unidentified.
  2. Whether the ÖNB Porträtsammlung's GZ00337643 is the impression Friis records as seen in Vienna in 1868 — a question for the Bildarchiv.
  3. Whether the ex-Kunstindustrimuseet impression described by Friis survives at Designmuseum Danmark.
  4. Westergaard I:204, entry 1409, has not been read at first hand; no digitisation exists.
  5. Friis's promised side-by-side reproductions of the print and KKSgb5254 — the best extant lever on the plate-versus-design question — have not been located.
  6. Friis's 'gloves in the left hand' reading of the pose has not been checked against KKSgb5254.
  7. The 'Non satis placet…' quotation has not been collated verbatim against TBOO VII, p. 373.
The 1595 portrait medal — the only likeness in metal
  1. Georg Galster, Danske og norske Medailler og Jetons (Copenhagen 1936), no. 53, p. 40, which carries an illustration, remains unread; it is not digitised.
  2. The Copenhagen specimen's inventory number, and whether it is a strike or an aftercast — known only to Den kgl. Mønt- og Medaillesamling.
  3. The 1852 Beschreibung der bisher bekannten böhmischen Privatmünzen und Medaillen (Prague, 1852), p. 16, Tab. III no. 17 — the last unseen primary source in the Prague/Vienna chain and the source of the rejected 'Opus Factum' reading of the obverse's O F.
  4. Whether the KHM Münzkabinett, Vienna, still holds the ex-k.k. larger specimen.
  5. Whether the reverse distich 'Arma, genus, fundi pereunt …' has a neo-Latin antecedent — unchecked, and prior to any claim about its authorship.
The aetatis-50 portrait (Skokloster / Edinburgh) — the exile likeness
  1. The Edinburgh band: FREMAT (Crompton's explicitly conjectural 1876 reconstruction) or STREPAT (as on Skokloster). It has never been read with certainty; conservation-grade imaging from the Royal Observatory Edinburgh, or examination in person, would settle it.
  2. Does the Gripsholm copy (NMGrh 1580) carry the emblem panel at all, and what does its band read?
  3. TBOO IX.211's numeral has not been verified against the printed page image — the last open thread under the 1597 year claim.
  4. Skokloster's support, canvas or wood: only the object, or a current conservation report, can settle it.
  5. The Bodleian's own record of its copy has not been consulted.
  6. Art UK's 81 × 75.6 cm for the Edinburgh canvas is unconfirmed against the Observatory's own cataloguing.
  7. The Collectio Lengertziana in Malmö (Ståhlbrand 1946) — several hundred Tycho writings and over a hundred graphic portraits, entirely unsurveyed here.
  8. What hangs in bays I–III of the Tycho Brahe museum on Ven is unrecorded; bays IV and V hold backlit reproduction panels, not paintings. Landskrona museum administers the collections.
The painted portraits in the finest Mechanica copies — Tycho between Ptolemy and Copernicus
  1. Where do Mortensen's 'Aet: LI' and Vellev's 'LII' actually sit — on Andersen's 1886 etching (Westergaard 1451), or on the Strahov, KVA or Kalocsa leaf? None has been inspected at reading resolution.
  2. Strahov holds a third Tycho watercolour, and it is of a different type — see the full-figure record. Whether the Strahov Mechanica's own watercolour matches the Copenhagen composition is now partially checked: the Studnička 1901 plate (p. 47) shows the Strahov leaf as a bust in an oval with an architectural/strapwork surround, two small flanking figures, a cartouche-like element above, and an arms roundel below — structurally cognate with Copenhagen, but the photogravure cannot confirm lettering or figure identities. A modern image from Strahov is still needed.
The posthumous copies (1602 onward)
  1. Per-copy portrait survey: the four Copenhagen (KB) Progymnasmata copies, the 1603/1610 issue-states, and surviving 1601 and 1610 Epistolarum witnesses — decisive for the titelauflage soft point (whether the 1610 reissue could have required a fresh portrait pull).
  2. Digitaler Portraitindex records 41–91 remain unexamined — the counterexample sweep is not closed.
  3. Thieme-Becker Band XXIX Sadeler entry (pp. 299–303) unopened — the one standard authority on the Sadelers not yet consulted.
  4. Date of the 'Marco Sadeler excudit' address on New Hollstein 238 State II — New Hollstein gives none.
  5. Portrait-leaf mechanics of the Epistolarum issues (integral title-verso versus inserted plate leaf) — decides how absolute the titelauflage reading of the 1610 reissue is.
  6. Models of the two unattributed LWL Münster woodcuts and of the Freher 1688 Theatrum book-portrait — undetermined.
  7. Whether the 1654 Paris first printing of Gassendi's Vita carries any portrait — the van Meurs plate is documented only in Vlacq's Hague 1655 second edition, and this record's 1655 end-date and icon-gassendi-frontispiece must agree on that basis.
The Kågeröd epitaph, 1613 — Tycho kneeling among his parents' children
  1. The full text of Regionmuseet Skåne KrM 2022:40 and the 2011 Skånes Målerikonservatorer condition assessment — the untested paint stratigraphy; until one is read, the reported total loss of the 1613 face has never been checked against physical evidence.
  2. The Danmarks Adels Aarbog XII (1895) plate — whether it shows the repainted state, as Mortensen's characterisation implies but no one has verified, and whether it carries a draughtsman or restorer credit.
  3. Nationalmuseet DMR-193014 at full resolution (6075 × 7415 px, never visually inspected) — and whether it is identical with the 'Tegning' Mortensen cites: its medium field reads 'photograph'.
  4. Whether the gold label 'Lerde Tyge B.' is 1613 lettering or renewed.
Tycho's portrait on the celestial globes — a distribution channel begun in his lifetime
  1. First-generation photography of HMF X 14609, which only the Historisches Museum Frankfurt can supply: the single gate on the miniature's model (second-engraving design or a reduced first engraving, held at medium confidence), the unread right-hand cartouche, the States-General privilege term (ad decem read against Mosley's fifteen years), and pixel confirmation of ÆTATIS 47 on the object.
  2. Whether the Houghton gore fragment of Blaeu's first celestial globe (9 of 12 gores, *51-2459 PF) includes the portrait zone — Warner, Imago Mundi 25 (1971) 29–38 is the arbiter (paywalled), and the call number itself has no live catalogue record (a reference query to Houghton would close both).
  3. The Plancius–Hondius globe of 1597/98: Secondary sources put 'a portrait of Tycho below Cetus' in that globe's tradition, possibly by conflation with its 1602 Blaeu copy or the 1613 globe. Test object: the twelve gores found 1983 in the Nicolai Collection, Württembergische Landesbibliothek Stuttgart. If real, the lifetime portrait-globe census has two or three members, not one, and the 1613 Aetatis-47 legend may descend via the Hondius house.
  4. The date of Blaeu's 10-cm celestial globe (Mosley: 'in the intervening period', with a Tycho attribution) — the one live remaining lifetime-globe candidate; van der Krogt 1993 pp. 503–505.
  5. The month of the Hondius 1601 globe (Tycho died 24 October 1601) — its lifetime status is genuinely undetermined.
Eckersberg's sketch, 1831 — the young Christian IV visiting Hven
  1. The sheet's inventory number, accession history, medium and dimensions, which only Frederiksborg can supply — the one remaining route from attested to located.
  2. Is the sheet a preparatory drawing, or a transfer/derivative drawing of the print campaigns — including its relation to the 18 June 1842 block drawing for Flinch?
  3. Villadsen's 2009 edition of Eckersberg's diaries (print only) and Fischer 1983, Tegninger af C.W. Eckersberg, remain unconsulted.
  4. The 27 February 2018 auction of the 26 × 17 cm oil (probably Hannover no. 441) is unidentified; the auction house's record is not public.
  5. Post-1992 whereabouts of the painting (Hannover no. 438) after Helge Pedersen.
The Levy memorial sheet — Tycho for children
  1. 'A. Levy' is unidentified in the Danish trade record — Kraks Vejviser c. 1855–1905, Dansk Bogfortegnelse, Dansk Boghandlertidende, the Royal Library's Småtrykssamling; other Levy first names should be tried, since 'A.' alone is thin.
  2. A full-text search of the verse lines in Mediestream would give a terminus post quem for the poem independent of the publisher question; the Copenhagen papers of October–December 1901 may carry advertisements for Tycho commemorative sheets, and the Nationaltidende article of 23 September 1901 has not been read.
  3. Whether the Antikvarisk-Topografisk Arkiv holds the physical sheet or a photograph only, under what journal or inventory number, and whether a high-resolution scan can be had.
  4. The intermediate nineteenth-century print behind the bare-headed oval bust is untraced; it would give an independent terminus post quem.
  5. Mortensen's 1949 iconography survey is unchecked, as is whether any register of Alfred Jacobsen's 'Danske Billeder' documents a no. 3.
The full-figure watercolour at Strahov — the standing Tycho with a sextant
  1. Is Studnička's wrapper a print from Šafařík's 1881 plate, or a fresh 1901 reproduction of the same original? Studnička's text is silent.
  2. Studnička's catalogue documents the Strahov Mechanica copy in detail but never mentions this Progymnasmata volume or the 1881 episode. The silence is now partially explained: his item C.I footnote borrows an illustration from a Progymnasmata, 1603, at Strahov, so a copy was accessible to him in 1900–01 — which weakens any inference that the volume had left Strahov by 1901 — and his catalogue's scope (ownership-relics of Tycho's own library) plausibly excludes a presentation gift, though that genre explanation is a candidate reading, not established. Whether the accessible copy is the same volume is unknown.
  3. No sighting of the Progymnasmata volume after 1881 has been located; Boldan & Vackářová's Strahov-focused Tycho chapter (2024) adds a third independent silence. Whether it is still at Strahov is unverified — the live-record check against Katalog Tritius and Manuscriptorium remains unexecuted, not negative.
  4. A comparison of the wrapper against Kilian's plate at higher resolution than a 1901 halftone allows: the direction of dependence is still open, and the halftone is too coarse to settle it.
Kilian's standing Tycho (1666) — a whole body invented around a borrowed head
  1. The plate's number in Westergaard, Danske portrætter i kobberstik (1930–33) — still not established. Only entries 1375–1382 have been consulted, short of any Kilian entry, and no number is inferred.
  2. What the reproductions in Studnička 1901 (pp. 44–48) and the 1901 wrapper actually depict, at caption level — the question that gates every surrogate comparison of the plate against the Strahov watercolour.
  3. The identity of 'Kraus' (Prague, before 1901), and Šafařík's 1881 account at its primary source.
  4. A live Strahov record for the Progymnasmata/Zajíc copy has not been reached; the post-1901 fate of both volume and watercolour is untested.
  5. The instrument's typological identity — no graduated arc is visible, and no positive match to a Mechanica plate has been made.
  6. Hollstein's Kilian volumes, for a systematic survey of his signature practice; Reznicek I p. 303 (the Schwarz portrait) unread.

9Sources

Every source cited by a record, in one alphabetical list: short form, full reference, what it contributes to this edition, the records that cite it, and a verified link where an open-access scan or the institution’s own record exists. The editor’s own unpublished research notes are listed as such and never as external authority. Three works that would settle more than most remain unread or unlocated: Mortensen’s note of 1935 on the oldest portrait, Schepelern’s catalogue of the Odense convent’s portraits (1959), and Wanscher’s Kronborgs Historie (1939); Beckett and Christensen (1921), Studnička (1901), Karl Madsen and Beckett (1897) have no open-access scan that could be verified, and carry no link.

  1. Beckett & Christensen, Uraniborg og Stjærneborg (1921)
    Francis Beckett & Charles Christensen, Tyge Brahes Uraniborg og Stjærneborg paa Hven (1921). Their conjecture that the engraving commission went 'to Goltzius, who passed it to his pupil de Gheyn' is superseded (its premises refuted); their architectural-image genealogy stands.
    Cited by 6 records: The Stjerneborg astronomer cycle · The mural quadrant portrait (Uraniborg) · De Gheyn's first engraving (Westergaard 1377) · The Copenhagen pen drawing (SMK KKSgb5254) · The 1595 portrait medal · The printed views of Uraniborg and Stjerneborg
  2. Blaeu, Grooten Atlas (1664) — the Hven instrument plates
    Joan Blaeu, Grooten Atlas, oft Werelt-beschryving (Amsterdam 1664), the Hven/Uraniborg section: the copper re-engravings of the Mechanica instrument figures with Dutch text, incl. both small equatorial armillaries (the steel-braced one, and the portrait-pedestal one under 'Armillae S') and the mural-quadrant interior. Examined in the Utrecht University exemplar (images 187–222 of its digitisation); the portrait-armillary plate is reproduced in the armillary-pedestal record.
    Cited by 1 record: The portraits on the equatorial armillary's pedestal
  3. Boldan & Vackářová, Bibliotheca astronomica (2024)
    Kamil Boldan & Jana Vackářová, Bibliotheca astronomica (Prague: Národní knihovna ČR, 2024), pp. 88–92 — the Strahov-focused Tycho chapter: illustrates the Mechanica presentation copy under its current shelfmark AG XI 56, and returns no trace of the Hasenburk Progymnasmata volume or its watercolour — the strongest and latest of the three independent silences since the 1881 report.
    Cited by 1 record: The full-figure watercolour at Strahov
  4. Bruun Hansen, 'Da Tycho Brahe gik i kloster' (2013)
    Gorm Bruun Hansen, 'Da Tycho Brahe gik i kloster — eller et billedes historie', Historisk Årbog for Roskilde Amt (2013), pp. 27–32 — the dedicated study of the Jensen copy at Roskilde Kloster: Høyen's 1830 Odense inspection, the three-stage 1835/1837/1838 removal sequence, and the Schepelern-derived discussion of the picture's descent into the Odense collection. Used critically: its 86 × 69 cm caption figure is a photo-credit carry-over from Tycho Brahes Verden 2006, not an independent measurement.
    Cited by 1 record: The Frederiksborg oil (attributed to Gemperlin)
    Open copy open access
  5. CERL Thesaurus, Lambert Cornelisz (cnp02079345)
    CERL Thesaurus record cnp02079345 (data.cerl.org/thesaurus/cnp02079345): Lambert Cornelisz, Amsterdam engraver of portraits and title pages, active 1593–1621, with 'Cornely' among the attested name variants (alongside Cornelis/Cornelii/Cornelij/Cornelius) and Jacques de Gheyn II recorded, without date, as his teacher. Record confirmed July 2026.
    Cited by 1 record: Lambert Cornelisz's print (Westergaard 1409)
    Open copy the record
  6. Christianson, On Tycho's Island (2000)
    John Robert Christianson, On Tycho's Island: Tycho Brahe and his Assistants, 1570–1601 (Cambridge, 2000), biographical directory: Jacques de Gheyn II (p. 291), Cornelius, Lambert (pp. 273–74), Gemperle, Crol. His c.1586–87 dating of the first engraving and the stamp-copies-engraving account are corrected by the letters and the Maestlin keystone.
    Cited by 12 records: The portraits on the equatorial armillary's pedestal · The Stjerneborg astronomer cycle · The Frederiksborg oil (attributed to Gemperlin) · The woodcut 'Effigies' (Westergaard 1376) · The mural quadrant portrait (Uraniborg) · De Gheyn's first engraving (Westergaard 1377) · De Gheyn's second engraving (Westergaard 1394) · Lambert Cornelisz's print (Westergaard 1409) · The small gilt brass quadrant · The printed views of Uraniborg and Stjerneborg · The posthumous copies (1602 onward) · Tycho's portrait on the celestial globes
  7. Christianson, Tycho Brahe and the Measure of the Heavens (2020)
    John Robert Christianson, Tycho Brahe and the Measure of the Heavens (London: Reaktion, 2020), pp. 31–32: the Basel-built quadrant described as a different, larger object (1–1.5 m radius, unstable in wind); the c.1568–69 Basel attribution for the emblem-quadrant of On Tycho's Island Fig. 51 is not repeated — the caption's date is silently dropped.
    Cited by 3 records: The portraits on the equatorial armillary's pedestal · The Frederiksborg oil (attributed to Gemperlin) · The small gilt brass quadrant
  8. Chytraeus, Variorum in Europa itinerum deliciae (Herborn, 1594)
    Nathan Chytraeus, Variorum in Europa itinerum deliciae (Herborn, 1594), pp. 596–598 — prints all eight distichs of the Stjerneborg astronomer cycle, including Tychonides, under a heading asserting that the painted icons with gold-lettered distichs exist in the hypocaustum; the preface states the Danish material came largely direct from Tycho. The earliest printed witness to the completed cycle, two years before the Epistolae astronomicae — reporting Tycho's own account, not autopsy. Direct inspection via Internet Archive (variorumineuropa00chyt_0).
    Cited by 2 records: The portraits on the equatorial armillary's pedestal · The Stjerneborg astronomer cycle
    Open copy open access
  9. Danmarks Adels Aarbog — the Bille-Brahe-Selby genealogy (via finnholbek.dk)
    Danmarks Adels Aarbog, the Bille-Brahe-Selby genealogy (DAA 1985-87:561-23c and related entries, as transcribed at finnholbek.dk, persons I11378 and I11368) — anchors Hendrik lensgreve Bille-Brahe-Selby's death on 7 May 1938, and establishes that no family member named Preben died in 1938 and none combined 'Baron' with 'Preben'. danmarkshistorien.lex.dk gives the death year as 1937, recorded as a minority reading.
    Cited by 1 record: The Steensgaard panel
  10. Danske Magazin II (Copenhagen, 1746) — the 1595 medal (pp. 165–168) and the supralibros account with the de Lode plate (pp. 321–322)
    Danske Magazin, vol. II (Copenhagen, 1746) — two separate first-hand Tycho loci in one volume. (1) p. 161 (the engraved plate) and pp. 165–168 (the medal discussion with notes 1–4) — the earliest published account of the 1595 medal. The compilers state that they personally inspected two 'Originaler' (one silver in the Royal Coin Cabinet, one base-metal in Klevenfeldt's collection) and describe specimens as 'afstemplede' (struck); note 4 records the Elephant 'hengende i en Kiæde' on the bust; the editors offer an explicitly unprovable twin conjecture on the occasion (the completion of the star catalogue and the great brass globe reaching usable form in 1595) and float Tycho's nephew Tage Ottesen Tott, then 15–16, as a possible commissioner. (2) 23. Hefte, pp. 321–322, 'Endnu videre Følge af rare og mest utrykte Efterretninger om Tyge Brahe' (Langebek) — the earliest published account of the supralibros, with an engraved plate of the portrait die on p. 321 signed 'O.H.d.Lode sc.', i.e. Odvard Helmoldt de Lode, the Copenhagen engraver (the attribution to him is conventional, not corpus-attested). The text states that Tycho 'har han selv ladet det i et Stempel udgrave, og brugt samme til at lade i Guld aftrykke uden paa Bindet af sine Skrifter'; that the plate was drawn from the Royal Library's blue-satin ('blaat Atlask') Mechanica (Wandsbek 1598) given 'd. 18. Decemb. 1600' to 'en forneme Herre i Bømen', i.e. LN 432 2°, eks. 2, the Petr Vok z Rožmberka copy; and that the plate is printed at the size of the stamp — 'Billedet paa Bogen er i selv samme Størrelse, som dette Koberstykke'. Page 322 continues: the second chain carries 'tydeligen … en Elefant' (an elephant, no order named); 'om der har været noget Smykke i den tredie, kand man ikke see'; the lower board carries the arms die 'i selv samme Form og Størrelse'; and the arms couplet is printed in the variant reading 'Arma, Genus, Fundi pereunt: Durabile Virtus / Et Doctrinæ decus Nobilitatis habent'.
    Cited by 3 records: The gilt portrait supralibros · The 1595 portrait medal · The Brahe coat-of-arms supralibros
    Open copy open access
  11. Dekker, 'Globes in Renaissance Europe' (History of Cartography vol. 3) — the census appendix
    Elly Dekker, 'Globes in Renaissance Europe,' in The History of Cartography, vol. 3 pt. 1 (Chicago UP), with Appendix 6.1, the census of Renaissance globes (chapter PDF at press.uchicago.edu, read and spot-checked verbatim, August 2026). Entries verified: no. 130 (van Langren celestial, 1586, 32.5 cm, Linköping Stifts- och Landsbiblioteket), no. 137 (1589, London NMM), no. 148 (1594, Frankfurt Historisches Museum — references Holbrook 1983, 69–73; Van der Krogt 1993, 424–26 and 429; Glasemann 1999, 15–20 — resolving Mosley's '424–426' as pages, not catalogue numbers), no. 161 (celestial gores, Willem Jansz. Blaeu, 1597, 34 cm, Houghton Library — a year against Mosley's 1598, both citing the same van der Krogt pages). The three surviving copies of the van Langren 32.5-cm celestial line documented here supersede Christianson 2000's 'No copy, however, has survived' (pp. 310–311).
    Cited by 1 record: Tycho's portrait on the celestial globes
    Open copy open access
  12. Det Nationalhistoriske Museum på Frederiksborg — published catalogue (frederiksborg.dk)
    The museum's published online catalogue at frederiksborg.dk, exhaustively enumerated via its four kunstvaerk sitemaps (c. 1,791 artwork records, July 2026): no Eckersberg drawing and no Christian IV/Tycho sheet; the only Tycho-adjacent holding published is Heinrich Hansen's A 153 (Vedel presenting the ballad collection to Queen Sophie — Christian IV not among the recorded sitters). The museum states institutionally that it owns drawings, prints and photographic portraits that cannot be permanently exhibited for conservation reasons — the published catalogue is a selection, so its negative characterises the publication, not the collection.
    Cited by 1 record: Eckersberg's sketch, 1831
  13. Det Nationalhistoriske Museum, Frederiksborg, object record A 155
    Frederiksborg museum catalogue record inv. A 155 ('Gravsten', maker 'Steffensen', dated 1885–1886, Room 27, person-tagged to Tycho Brahe; frederiksborg.dk) — the structured record never names Prague or the Týn church, but its catalogue photograph shows the exact Týn composition, and the 1937 Rundetaarn jubilee volume independently confirms a Frederiksborg cast; the A 155 = Týn identification is well-supported inference, not documentary.
    Cited by 1 record: The Týn church epitaph, Prague
    Open copy the record
  14. Detmold Mechanica, Lippische Landesbibliothek Nl 70.4° (IIIF)
    Lippische Landesbibliothek Detmold, hand-coloured Astronomiae instauratae mechanica, Nl 70.4°, IIIF manifest digitale-sammlungen.llb-detmold.de/i3f/v20/2547750/manifest — the quadrans minor plate at canvas 2547769 (leaf [16], 4173 × 3223 px), Tycho's Explicatio at canvas 2547770. The only public deep-zoom surrogate of the plate; first-hand inspection (July 2026) shows the emblem roundel as uncoloured line-work within an otherwise richly hand-coloured plate, and most of Tycho's verbal programme not legible on the plate.
    Cited by 1 record: The small gilt brass quadrant
    Open copy the record
  15. Dreyer's Opera Omnia X preface (1923), via Meddelelser fra Ole Rømers Venner (2006)
    J. L. E. Dreyer's preface to Tychonis Brahe Opera Omnia X (1923), cited via Meddelelser fra Ole Rømers Venner 14/1 (2006), p. 31 — Dreyer's second, unhedged endorsement of the Strahov watercolour as the model for Kilian's plate; like his 1890 endorsement it roots in the single 1881 Šafařík report.
    Cited by 1 record: The full-figure watercolour at Strahov
  16. Dreyer, Tycho Brahe (1890) — the medal and the life
    J. L. E. Dreyer, Tycho Brahe: a picture of scientific life and work in the sixteenth century (Edinburgh 1890), ch. 9 and n. 66: the two variants of the 1595 medal, their legends and sizes, a silver specimen in the royal collection at Copenhagen and another at Prague, citing Danske Magazin ii p. 161 and Weistritz ii p. 14.
    Cited by 7 records: The portraits on the equatorial armillary's pedestal · The mural quadrant portrait (Uraniborg) · The 1595 portrait medal · The aetatis-50 portrait (Skokloster / Edinburgh) · The small gilt brass quadrant · The full-figure watercolour at Strahov · Kilian's standing Tycho (1666)
    Open copy open access
  17. Eller, Det nationalhistoriske Museum på Frederiksborg 1878–1978 (1978)
    Povl Eller, Det nationalhistoriske Museum på Frederiksborg 1878–1978 (Hillerød, 1978), esp. p. 43 — the museum's centennial history: the founding-era history-painting programme (J. C. Jacobsen's foundation, royal decree of 5 April 1878; abandoned c. 1890) and the Stue 27 account of Hansen's 1882 Uraniborg reconstruction, naming Vedel, Queen Sophie and Tycho. A narrative centennial guide, not an inventory catalogue: it names no commissioner, price, or terms for any single canvas, never mentions A 154, and uses no accession-number notation.
    Cited by 1 record: Heinrich Hansen, 1882
  18. Fortegnelse over indbo fra Steensgaard (V. Winkel & Magnussen, 1938) — the dispersal catalogue
    Fortegnelse over indbo fra Steensgaard (Copenhagen: V. Winkel & Magnussen, auction Thursday 20 October 1938, 48 pp.) — the sale that dispersed the Steensgaard contents after Hendrik lensgreve Bille-Brahe-Selby's death. Known so far only through a bookseller's listing (Gurra's Books, Hemse, item #026711): a located lead, not yet an examined anchor — the listing's title-page transcription names the decedent 'Baron Preben Bille-Brahe-Selby', conflicting on both forename and title with the documented Hendrik lensgreve; most likely a transcription error, but unresolved until the physical title page is seen.
    Cited by 1 record: The Steensgaard panel
  19. Fr. Beckett, Renaissancen og Kunstens Historie i Danmark (1897)
    Francis Beckett, Renaissancen og Kunstens Historie i Danmark (Copenhagen 1897), pp. 206, 212–13 — the earliest art-historical case for the Gemperlin attribution of the Frederiksborg oil, argued from Jensen's copy against the 1586 engraving and a sketch then under Goltzius's name (derivatives compared to derivatives, never to the signed Vedel 1578 or St Ibb 1578 anchors); records Høyen's 1830 Odense inspection from his Notitsbog.
    Cited by 1 record: The Frederiksborg oil (attributed to Gemperlin)
  20. Friis, Bidrag til dansk Kunsthistorie (1890–1901)
    F. R. Friis, Bidrag til dansk Kunsthistorie (Copenhagen 1890–1901): the earliest detailed eyewitness description of the Copenhagen pen drawing (p. 222, recording no inscription on it), the Lambert Cornelisz print (p. 228, his reading 'Cornay'), and the painted Mechanica portrait with the Ptolemy and Copernicus statues and the angels' cartouche (p. 238).
    Cited by 13 records: The Steensgaard panel · The Frederiksborg oil (attributed to Gemperlin) · The Prague panel · The mural quadrant portrait (Uraniborg) · The gilt portrait supralibros · The Copenhagen pen drawing (SMK KKSgb5254) · Lambert Cornelisz's print (Westergaard 1409) · The 1595 portrait medal · The aetatis-50 portrait (Skokloster / Edinburgh) · The painted portraits in the finest Mechanica copies · The printed views of Uraniborg and Stjerneborg · The full-figure watercolour at Strahov · Kilian's standing Tycho (1666)

  21. Friis, Tyge Brahe, en historisk Fremstilling (1871)
    F. R. Friis, Tyge Brahe, en historisk Fremstilling af hans Liv og videnskabelige Virksomhed (Copenhagen, 1871), p. 298 — the Týn monument raised by the heirs 'nogle Aar senere', after Tycho's wife Kirstine had also found her resting place in the church (1604): the earliest of the three concurring attestations that the erection followed her death, and the source of the record's terminus post quem.
    Cited by 2 records: The 1595 portrait medal · The Týn church epitaph, Prague
  22. Frombork Scientific Inventory — Tycho Brahe: Timepieces
    'Scientific Inventory — Tycho Brahe: Timepieces', Nicolaus Copernicus Museum-affiliated inventory (frombork.art.pl), listing 'Clock, 1623, National Museum, Copenhagen (Inv no. D 138)' — the competing unfooted date of 1623 for the console-clock plates, with a description matching the Nationalmuseet record; no source stated. That the inventory draws on genuine Nationalmuseet data is cross-checked on the adjacent D125 entry (the falsely inscribed 'Tycho Brahe 1597' pocket watch) against the museum's own record.

  24. Gassendi, Tychonis Brahei Vita (Paris 1654) — the unsigned state
    Pierre Gassendi, Tychonis Brahei… Vita (Paris: Vidua Mathurini Dupuis, 1654), the first edition, with the portrait bound facing Book One.   25. Gattei, 'The Engraved Frontispiece of Kepler's Tabulae Rudolphinae' (2009)
    Stefano Gattei, 'The Engraved Frontispiece of Kepler's Tabulae Rudolphinae (1627): A Preliminary Study', Nuncius XXIV.2 (2009), 341–365 — a panel-by-panel iconographic inventory of the frontispiece. Names Georg Celer (Cöler) of Nuremberg as the engraver, from the plate itself (p. 363); attributes the preliminary sketch (Vienna ÖNB Cod. 9737d, Bl. 30) 'most probably' to Schickard (p. 346); documents the heirs' specification of formal dress and the Elephant regalia (pp. 348, 354). Silent throughout on any de Gheyn model for the Tycho figure — a silence that favours, but does not prove, an independent lineage.
    Cited by 2 records: The posthumous copies (1602 onward) · The 1642 console clock

  26. Gigas, 'Nogle Bøger fra Tycho Brahes Bogbinderi' (Bogvennen, 1900)
    Emil Gigas, 'Nogle Bøger fra Tycho Brahes Bogbinderi', Bogvennen (Copenhagen, 1900) — the earliest detailed description of Tycho's presentation bindings (materials, the two gold ovals + mottoes, the rhombus-and-lily layout, the inside de Gheyn portrait) and twelve KB copies + the foreign copies.
    Cited by 2 records: The gilt portrait supralibros · The inserted author portrait in Epistolae and Mechanica copies
  27. Grinder-Hansen (ed.), Tycho Brahes Verden (Nationalmuseet, 2006)
    Poul Grinder-Hansen (ed.), Tycho Brahes Verden: Danmark i Europa 1550–1600 (Copenhagen: Nationalmuseet, 2006) — the exhibition volume. An exhibition catalogue rather than a specialist iconography: its captions are the fullest modern account of several of these objects, but it carries identifiable errors (the Gemperlin portrait dated 'formentlig o. 1580'; an astrolabe in the brass-quadrant emblem where Tycho's Latin says a celestial globe and a book; a copperplate 'dated 1586' as the model for the first engraving, of which none is known). Cite it for what it photographs, check it for what it asserts.
    Cited by 18 records: The portraits on the equatorial armillary's pedestal · The Stjerneborg astronomer cycle · The Frederiksborg oil (attributed to Gemperlin) · The gilt portrait supralibros · Frederik II's funeral procession, 1588 · The 1595 portrait medal · The aetatis-50 portrait (Skokloster / Edinburgh) · The Týn church epitaph, Prague · The Brahe coat-of-arms supralibros · The small gilt brass quadrant · The posthumous copies (1602 onward) · The Kågeröd epitaph, 1613 · Tycho's portrait on the celestial globes · The 1642 console clock · Heinrich Hansen, 1882 · Eckersberg's sketch, 1831 · The Levy memorial sheet · The full-figure watercolour at Strahov

    Emil Hannover, Maleren C. W. Internal page references for nos. 438/441 conflict across retrieval routes — cite by entry number, not page.

    Ivana Horáček, 'Illuminating Methods, Picturing Instruments', Austrian History Yearbook 52 (2021), pp. 30–53 — names three instrument images as new 1598 engravings in the Mechanica. Consulted in abstract only; the full text remains paywalled.

  30. Icones instrumentorum (Uraniborg 1596, Nielsen 975) — the block's first issue
    Icones instrumentorum quorundam astronomiae instaurandae gratia a Tychone Brahe Dano...elaboratorum (Uraniborg, [1596]): 18 letterpress-free woodcut pulls of the instrument blocks, issued as a supplement with some copies of the 1596 Epistolae astronomicae; presentation copies to Christian IV and Chancellor Friis December 1596 (Norlind p. 235; Mosley App. I; Dreyer p. 230 n. 2). The armillary leaf prints the Fig. 12 block with the full portrait pedestal — examined in the Stockholm KB exemplar's digitisation (August 2026): same-block verdict on total design and letter-key correspondence. Four copies are censused (Stockholm KB, Vienna, Wrocław, and one in the trade).

  33. Jern, Uraniborg — herresäte och himlaborg (Lund 1976)
    Carl Henrik Jern, Uraniborg — herresäte och himlaborg (Lund 1976), p. 139 (printed p. 142) n. 53 — cites Wanscher, Kronborgs Historie (1939), pp. 64–65, for the tapestry figure, but paraphrases him as wearing the Order of the Elephant ('behängd med elefantordén'), the opposite of Wanscher's own argument as quoted verbatim by Mortensen 1946. Cited here for the contradiction, not for the costume.

  34. Karl Madsen, Kunstens Historie i Danmark (c. 1901–07)
    Karl Madsen, Kunstens Historie i Danmark, pp. 62–64 — the fullest published analysis of the Næsbyholm painted ceiling, identifying the border medallions as the squire's own family and dating the painting to the 1590s rather than to the 1585 building campaign.

  35. Karlson, Tobias Gemperlin och några skånska 1500-talsporträtt (Lund 1928)
    William Karlson, Tobias Gemperlin och några skånska 1500-talsporträtt (Lund 1928) — the painter-attribution monograph on Gemperlin and the Danish–Scanian portrait group. Note 24 attests that Hvedholm portraits were transferred to Frederiksborg in 1923 'og erstattedes på Hvedholm af kopier' (and were replaced at Hvedholm by copies) — the mechanism that keeps a 1923 arrival at Frederiksborg live as an alternative route for A 8191. A monograph, not a census: its silence on any Tycho panel is non-diagnostic.

A higher-resolution surrogate should be examined before the negative is treated as final.

  45. Mosley, Bearing the Heavens (Cambridge, 2007)
    Adam Mosley, Bearing the Heavens: Tycho Brahe and the Astronomical Community of the Late Sixteenth Century (Cambridge, 2007), p. 249 — the globe-makers' portraits: Tycho supplied van Langren and Blaeu with impressions of his own engraved portrait, the same image used in the Epistolae, 'to grant legitimacy to the instruments'. The practice begins in 1594, in his lifetime and at his initiative.

  49. Norlind, Tycho Brahe (Lund 1970) — the Mechanica copy census
    Norlind, Tycho Brahe: en levnadsteckning (Lund 1970), pp. 289–291 — the institution-by-institution census of Mechanica copies transmitting Hasselberg's list; marks the watercolour portrait on exactly four copies (Copenhagen eks. 2 = Hasselberg nr 3, Strahov nr 23 'Med porträtt som i KVA:s', Stockholm KVA nr 1 = Wargentin, Kalocsa nr 22) and records a single Stockholm holding — the basis for resolving Mortensen's 'Royal Library in Stockholm' to the KVA copy. His placement of the KVA portrait 'på titelbladets verso-sida' is carried as possibly precise, possibly loose paraphrase. The same census independently anchors the reproductions in Studnička 1901, pp. 44–48, to the Strahov Mechanica presentation copy (today AG XI 56) — the second witness, beside Friis's Addenda, for the caution that surrogate comparisons of Kilian's plate against 'the Strahov watercolour' may be comparing the wrong watercolour.

    Ordbog over det danske Sprog, s.vv. 'ej' and 'smaa' (ordnet.dk/ods) — the historical dictionary of Danish. Consulted for the dating value of the Levy sheet's orthography: 'ei' is recorded as a deliberate literary archaism from c. 1650 onward, and 'smaae' fluctuates from the eighteenth century, so neither spelling discriminates within the sheet's 1855–1901 bounds. The 'ej' entry was confirmed; the 'smaa' entry's specific reform-date bounds could not be verified from the online page.

  62. Stevenson, Terrestrial and Celestial Globes (Yale, 1921) — the cartouche transcriptions
    Edward Luther Stevenson, Terrestrial and Celestial Globes: Their History and Construction, 2 vols. (New Haven: Yale UP for the Hispanic Society of America, 1921; Project Gutenberg 39866/39867, read in full, August 2026). Vol. II transcribes the Dutch celestial-globe cartouches: the 1600 Hondius 'globus Tychonicus' (title, Leiden/Franeker dedication, Cassiopeia nova legend — no portrait recorded), the 1601 Hondius 21-cm (figures agreeing with 1600, 'the chief difference lying merely in the matter of size'), the 1603 Blaeu (portrait of Tycho with the motto 'Non haberi, sed esse'), and the 1613 Veen/Hondius Jr. globe's portrait legend 'Effigies Nob. viri Tychonis Brahe Dani Domini de Knudstrup. Summi Mathematici. Aetatis 47.' (verified verbatim) — the frozen age-47 that points the copy-chain back to a 1593/94 model. Vol. I records the van Langren States-General privilege of 9 September 1592 renewed 1596 to Jacobus and his sons Arnoldus and Henricus (Stevenson's 'Estates of Amsterdam' for the 1592 grant conflicts with van der Krogt 1984's States-General; prefer van der Krogt). His 1914 Hispanic Society monograph on Blaeu (Gutenberg 75272) adds the Hven data-transactions: Arnold van Langren sent 1591 and refused the incomplete catalogue, Hendrick sent April 1593. Caveat: Stevenson's 'no Blaeu celestial globe earlier than 1602' is superseded by Warner 1971 and van der Krogt 1993 on the Houghton gores.

  66. The editor's research notes on the 1594 globe portrait (August 2026)
    The editor's unpublished research notes of August 2026 on the van Langren 1594 globe portrait: the ÆTATIS 47 cartouche read at object level from Mosley's fig. 4.7, with the full Effigies tablet, star-count scroll, imprint and privilege cartouche transcriptions; the withdrawal of the earlier claim that the miniature 'must descend from the first engraving' (a terminus ante quem misread as a post quem); the medium-confidence verdict, held against a second reading, that the miniature reverses the second design; the lifetime globe census (one confirmed portrait globe; Blaeu 1597/98 and Plancius–Hondius 1597/98 indeterminate; Hondius 1600/1601 negative). Supersedes the July 2026 notes on the globes where they conflict.

  67. The editor's research notes on the arms supralibros (July 2026)
    The editor's unpublished research notes of July 2026 on the coat-of-arms supralibros, the arms oval interrogated on its own terms: the matched-pair adjudication (zero single-oval bindings in some 25–30 examined objects), the 1584 apparatus adjudication, the first independent arms measurement (Stockholm KB De mundi RAR: 106 A, c. 6.6 × 5.3 cm on a transferred scale, medium confidence), and the Bodleian Savile De mundi resolved as a documented negative.

  70. Thoren, The Lord of Uraniborg (1990)
    Victor E. Thoren, The Lord of Uraniborg: A Biography of Tycho Brahe (Cambridge, 1990), pp. 75–78, 151 n. 17, 163 n. 39: the dating anchor for the small gilt quadrant — Tycho's log of the lunar eclipse of 8 December 1573 (TBOO X, 38–39) contains an unambiguous reference to this instrument, deployed against Dreyer's post-1576 Uraniborg claim; the emblem's programme linked to the Urania elegy of spring 1573; the instrument obsolete by c.1580 and never used again; the 'Q. min.' abbreviation in the observation logs denotes the Quadrans mediocris, not this instrument.

    Christianson, Hadravová, Hadrava, Šolc (eds), Tycho Brahe and Prague: Crossroads of European Science (Frankfurt am Main, 2002): Wolfschmidt, p. 208 — the destruction account is collective and category-limited (most of the high-accuracy instruments destroyed after 1619, only the two sextants surviving); Fučíková, pp. 279–280 — the 1607–1650 Prague Kunstkammer inventories held relatively few astronomical instruments, an anonymous low-valued group of quadrants and sextants, nothing matching this object.
    Cited by 2 records: The Prague panel · The small gilt brass quadrant
  72. Tycho Brahe, Astronomiae instauratae mechanica (Wandsbek, 1598)
    Tycho Brahe, Astronomiae instauratae mechanica (Wandsbek, 1598), TBOO V.56–60: the Fig. 12 equatorial armillary, its pedestal 'e solido et forti ligno', carrying the four icones of Ptolemy, Albategnius, Copernicus 'tum quoque NOSTRAM'; the Fig. 14 great armillary's steel base and portrait-less chapter. English in Raeder, Strömgren & Strömgren, Tycho Brahe's Description of his Instruments and Scientific Work (Copenhagen, 1946), pp. 56–59, 64. The chapter is also a Tycho-authored precedent for the Tycho/Copernicus paired-portrait scheme on decorated instrument surfaces ('those of Copernicus and myself, are seen at d'), the ancestor of the 1642 console-clock plates.
    Cited by 2 records: The portraits on the equatorial armillary's pedestal · The 1642 console clock
    Open copy open access
  73. Tycho Brahe, Astronomiæ Instauratæ Mechanica (1598) — the Quadrans Muralis chapter
    Tycho Brahe, Astronomiæ Instauratæ Mechanica (Wandsbek 1598), Quadrans Muralis chapter, in the DSL Tekstnet edition (ed. Zeeberg / Gredal Jensen): Tycho's own account of the mural painting — Gemperlin and 'Iohannes de Embdâ Stenwichel' named outright, the landscape hand only as 'Iohannes de Antverpiâ pictor Regius Coronaeburgensis'; his endorsement of the tablet's date ('prout vides') and his praise of the likeness ('vix similior dari possit').
    Cited by 1 record: The mural quadrant portrait (Uraniborg)
    Open copy open access
  74. Tycho Brahe, De mundi aetherei recentioribus phaenomenis (Uraniborg, 1588)
    Tycho Brahe, De mundi aetherei recentioribus phaenomenis liber secundus (Uraniborg, 1588) = Opera Omnia IV — the chapter heading 'NOVA MVNDANI SYSTEMATIS HYPOTYPOSIS AB AVTHORE NVPER ADINVENTA', verbatim-verified: the first-person ancestor of the clock plate's third-person banderole 'HYPOTYPOSIS Systematis mundani a Tychone inventi'.
    Cited by 1 record: The 1642 console clock
  75. Tycho Brahe, Epistolae astronomicae (Uraniborg, 1596)
    Tycho Brahe, Epistolarum astronomicarum libri (Uraniborg, 1596), pp. 243–246 = Opera Omnia VI.273–276 — Tycho's own printed description of the painted decoration of Stjerneborg's warming room, naming all eight astronomers and giving their distichs. A lifetime, first-person, printed source for a lost painting.
    Cited by 2 records: The Stjerneborg astronomer cycle · The 1642 console clock
  76. Tycho's own letters: the December 1590 dispatches; to Pontanus, 5 September 1595 (TBOO VII)
    Tycho Brahe, Epistolae astronomicae / Opera Omnia VII: to Thomas Savile 1 December 1590 ('nuper Amsterodami cupro insculptae', p. 285); to Heinrich Rantzau 7 December 1590 (the 'nimiis monilibus onustae' complaint and the re-order, pp. 300–301); to Scultetus 8 December 1590 (p. 302); to Johannes Pontanus 5 September 1595 (the Cornelisz print received and criticised, VII:371/373).
    Cited by 3 records: De Gheyn's first engraving (Westergaard 1377) · De Gheyn's second engraving (Westergaard 1394) · Lambert Cornelisz's print (Westergaard 1409)
  77. Tychonis Brahe Opera Omnia IX (Dreyer ed.)
    Tychonis Brahe Dani Opera Omnia, ed. J. L. E. Dreyer, vol. IX, pp. 175–78 — the press motto and the effigies and arms distichs printed under CIRCA EFFIGIEM TYCHONIS / CIRCA ARMA TYCHONIS, with the trailing apparatus note sourcing them from an exemplar printed by Tycho's own press, 'Uraniburgi 1584' (cf. Cod. Vindob. lat. 10686/7); Dreyer's note-after-item convention adjudicated from the surrounding pages.
    Cited by 1 record: The Brahe coat-of-arms supralibros
  78. Tychonis Brahe Opera Omnia — the quadrant's primary witnesses
    Tychonis Brahe Dani Opera Omnia (ed. Dreyer): V, 12–15, the Mechanica's quadrans minor chapter with Tycho's own description of the emblem ('Juvenis laurea insignitus', celestial globe and book); VI, 284–285, the 1591 instrument synopsis for Landgrave Wilhelm IV of Hessen-Kassel, item XXV — the earliest dated witness to the emblem's description, motto included; X, 38–39, the December 1573 eclipse log cited by Thoren as the dating anchor.
    Cited by 1 record: The small gilt brass quadrant
  79. Tyge Brahes Meteorologiske Dagbog (la Cour ed., 1876) — the Uraniborg diary
    Tyge Brahes Meteorologiske Dagbog holdt paa Uraniborg for Aarene 1582–1597, ed. P. la Cour (Copenhagen 1876), pp. 104–107: the entry 'Sepultura Regis' at Frederik II's burial, 5 June 1588, with no travel note for Tycho — against the diary's own convention of logging his journeys off Hven.
    Cited by 1 record: Frederik II's funeral procession, 1588
    Open copy open access
  80. Van Regteren Altena, Jacques de Gheyn: Three Generations (1983)
    I. Q. van Regteren Altena, Jacques de Gheyn: Three Generations (The Hague 1983): cat. II 653 (the lost design for the first engraving), 654 (the Copenhagen drawing — 'the design for De Gheyn's second engraving in reverse … traced with a needle for transfer', the 'false monogram HG'), 694 (the Marnix pen model), 677 (the Grotius drawing with Ploos van Amstel's false 'HG 1590'). His c.1595–96 dating of the engravings is corrected at the upper end by the Cornelisz terminus.
    Cited by 2 records: The Copenhagen pen drawing (SMK KKSgb5254) · De Gheyn's second engraving (Westergaard 1394)
  81. Vellev, 'Et industrianlæg og en votivsten på Hven …' (Renæssanceforum 12, 2017)
    Jens Vellev, 'Et industrianlæg og en votivsten på Hven …', Renæssanceforum 12 (2017) — peer-reviewed account of the Hven votive stone, whose paired Brahe (paternal) and Bille (maternal) arms independently confirm the blazon of the plain paternal shield, sable, a pale argent, carried alone on the supralibros.
    Cited by 1 record: The Brahe coat-of-arms supralibros
    Open copy open access
  82. Vierteljahrsschrift der Astronomischen Gesellschaft 16 (Leipzig, 1881), p. 273
    The minutes of the Astronomische Gesellschaft's Strasbourg assembly, item 7: 'Herr Safarik vertheilt an die Anwesenden Exemplare einer Photolithographie nach dem einzigen in Prag vorhandenen Originalporträt Tycho Brahe's. Dieses ist ein Aquarell in Folio auf Pergament, einem Exemplar der Astronomiae instauratae progymnasmata vorgebunden, mit autographer Dedication an den böhmischen Magnaten Johann Zajic von Hasenburk, und befindet sich gegenwärtig im Kloster Strahow zu Prag.' Read from the Bayerische Staatsbibliothek's digitisation (shelfmark Astr.u. 209 r-16, urn:nbn:de:bvb:12-bsb11555032-4), printed page 273 = scan 287. Note: the print reads 'befindet sich' (singular); Friis quotes it as 'befinden sich'.
    Cited by 2 records: The full-figure watercolour at Strahov · Kilian's standing Tycho (1666)
    Open copy open access
  83. Wellcome Collection catalogue, the photogravure sheet (1355i / V0000746)
    Wellcome Collection, London, catalogue record atbe9xn8 (no. 1355i / image V0000746): 'Tycho Brahe. Photogravure after J. Falck', citing R. Burgess, Portraits of Doctors & Scientists in the Wellcome Institute (1973), no. 405.7. Plate number 8279; IIIF native maximum 2200 × 3190 px; Public Domain Mark. The 'after J. Falck' title takes the sheet's lettering at face value; Burgess's entry itself remains unexamined.
    Cited by 1 record: The Prague panel
    Open copy the record
  84. Zeeberg, Den praktiske muse (1993)
    Peter Zeeberg, Den praktiske muse (1993), pp. 18–21 with n. 19 — reads the Tychonides figure as a 'tænkte arvtager' (notional heir), printing »Tychonides« in scare quotes.
    Cited by 1 record: The Stjerneborg astronomer cycle
